# Work that can be handed to a subagent

Each entry is meant to be pasted into a ticket more or less as it stands. Anything
without a way to *measure* whether it worked is not ready to hand out — write the
measurement first.

## Every ticket runs in its own git worktree

    tools/worktree.sh <name>

prints a directory, a branch, and the `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` to export. The agent works THERE and
commits to ITS OWN BRANCH; the coordinator merges back with `git merge --no-ff`. Nothing else
gets an agent's half-finished edits, and nobody has to remember not to build at the wrong moment.

Why this exists: on 2026-09-07 four separate incidents came from agents and coordinator sharing
one checkout. A build taken while a worker held `src/` compiled its half-finished file and
returned a normal-looking number; the same collision later made a build fail outright; and I told
a worker its correct measurement was contamination on the strength of one of those numbers.

What the script handles that a bare `git worktree add` does not:
- `reference/bn6f` is a submodule and a fresh worktree checks it out EMPTY. It is symlinked to
  the main checkout, because it is only ever READ -- disassembly comments are made by the
  coordinator in the main tree, so an agent must not commit inside it. The script marks the
  path `--skip-worktree` so that a stray `git add -A` cannot stage the symlink over the
  gitlink -- doing that once merged a self-referential symlink into main and deleted the
  disassembly from the working tree. Agents should stage by path regardless.
- Each worktree needs its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` or the builds serialise on one lock. A build in
  a fresh worktree takes about 30 seconds and produces a BYTE-IDENTICAL ROM to the main tree's --
  verified, three ways, so a number measured in a worktree is directly comparable to one measured
  anywhere else.
- Capture scratch paths are keyed on the checkout path by `chip_compare.scratch()`, so two agents
  cannot overwrite each other's packed ROM between its build and its capture. That needed no
  argument and no convention; it just works out.

An agent that commits on its own branch is also free to commit MIDWAY, which the old "never
commit" rule forbade -- and that rule existed only because everyone shared one tree.

## Rules for every ticket

- **Give each agent its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`** under `/tmp` (`tools/worktree.sh` prints one). `cargo` locks the shared
  target directory and every build overwrites the same `target/.../bn` that
  `tools/gbafix.py` reads, so two agents building at once will silently pack each
  other's ROM. This has already caused two wrong captures.
  This rule was itself a trap until 2026-09-07: `regress.py`'s `build()` hardcoded
  `ROOT/target/...`, so setting `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` made cargo build in one place and the
  ROM get packed from a stale ELF in another -- no error, just numbers for a different
  build (`mettaur` 95436 instead of 345). It now asks `cargo metadata` where the target
  directory actually is, so the rule and the harness finally agree. Any OTHER script that
  packs a ROM should be checked for the same assumption before being trusted under a
  private target dir.
- **Baseline first.** The agent must record the number with its own script *before*
  changing anything, and re-run the identical script afterwards, and report both. A
  change nobody can show improves a number is not a fix — see 7ah in `TRANSFER.md`,
  where a well-cited change backed by a disassembly line and the animation data turned
  out to move nothing and to be worse on a wider window.
- **Say which window was measured.** Two agents measuring the same residue in different
  frame windows reached opposite conclusions once. Windows go in the report.
- Only `src/` changes unless the ticket says otherwise. Never `tools/`, `reference/`,
  `assets/` or `Cargo.toml` without a reason in the report.
- A precise negative result is a good outcome and gets written into `TRANSFER.md`. A
  plausible-sounding change that does not move the number is not.
- **DO THE WORK YOURSELF.** Do not delegate a ticket onward to a further agent. You
  are the worker. A ticket that turns out to be bigger than it looked comes back as a
  report saying so, not as a subcontract. (Which model runs which role is decided in
  `.pi/agents/` and HANDOFF §13, not by the worker.)

## Rules for whoever is handing the tickets out

Written after breaking all three of these in one evening.

- **Do not build or measure from the tree while a worker holds `src/`.** Not just "do not edit" --
  a build taken mid-edit compiles whatever state the worker's file is in at that instant and gives
  a number that looks completely normal. This produced a `tiles` reading of 3295 and then one of 0
  within the hour, the second being a build with the worker's fix reverted. Reuse an already-built
  ROM from /tmp, or wait.
- **When a worker's number disagrees with yours, rebuild it yourself before contradicting them.**
  On the strength of the above I told a worker its correct measurement was contamination. It had
  done three from-scratch builds and was right. Its EXPLANATION was wrong (it blamed target-path
  length; three target directories, one deliberately long, give a byte-identical ROM) -- but a
  wrong explanation is not a wrong measurement, and the two must be judged separately.
- **`git add -u <path>` limits to that path.** A commit meant to carry the fix, the harness change
  and two write-ups carried only the submodule bump, and the rest sat unstaged behind a clean-
  looking `git status` line. Check `git show --stat` after committing, not just the exit code.

**Common to F8-F23 (and their b-tickets) unless the ticket says otherwise.** Baseline the row (harness line plus
`tools/diffmask.py` region, plus `tools/oracle.py` where the row is supported); localize the residue to
frames and an element; find canon's routine for that element in reference/bn6f and cite it; fix src/
(or the fixture/descriptor when the residue is the fixture, with `peeked` provenance); re-run the row,
wave, window, opening, chip-cannon and the full table -- nothing may get worse, and a row that does is
reported with its numbers. No allowlist change; no alignment or region change except by measured event
(F2's rule). **Coordinator:** verify_rows on every row the report names; the verifier only for claims
beyond harness lines; a PARTIAL from a wrong guess about the cause gets one follow-up ticket; if that
also fails, mark the ticket BLOCKED and move on to the next OPEN ticket.

**Result.** Poke mechanism discovered: --poke at 0x0203528F:0x01 does NOT set the byte (poke rounds to half-word 0x0203528E so the high byte lands at 0x0203528F and gets masked). Workaround verified: --poke 0x0203528E:0x0100 (16-bit half-word where high byte=1, low byte=0) sets byte 0x0203528F to 0x01 cleanly. ### T118. M2/display driver: make the cursor row's pass class a property of logic, not of the binary footprint — copy only the changed 8-word chunks and retire SEAM_PHASE_PAD_ITERS  *(NEGATIVE -- 2026-09-17, Branch wt/T118 kept UNMERGED [tip 18c2b96)*

**Result.** Branch wt/T118 kept UNMERGED (tip 18c2b96; the code experiment is commit ef784d6, reverted in the tip; do not merge ef784d6). The ticket's premise was wrong in an informative way and the ticket's own acceptance ('every other row byte-identical') is what killed it. WHAT WAS TESTED: agb's commit() copies the whole screenblock (vendor/agb .../screenblock.rs:42-47), so the changed-8-word-chunk-only copy was implemented and SEAM_PHASE_PAD_ITERS deleted (fitted 17 -> 16). RESULT: cursor 1/1/170/186279 -> 16187/3385/170 (negative 170611) and, the killer, windowclose 0/0/40 -> 63962/3557 (negative 266033) — a row that had been 0 for months. All 10 other rows byte-identical (opening/mettaur/cannon/buster/chip-use/wave/result/popup/emotion_syn 0, buster_charge still 2498/188/32). Step 5's three-footprint table was not run: acceptance had already failed, and the residue re-rolled between two code-size variants (15872/3235 vs 16187/3385), i.e. not stable. THE REAL FINDING (this is the deliverable): the census says volume was never the problem — a 32x32 map is 64 8-word chunks (2048 B) and each cursor move dirties 22 (~704 B); whole copy ~= 4.1k cycles ~= 3.3 scanlines against a 76,704-cycle vblank budget (68 lines x 1128), so the copy NEVER spilled past vblank; changed-only worst ~= 5.5k, also inside. The k-table shows the mechanism exactly: before, the only non-zero frame is k=97 (1 px); after, k=7/37/67/97/127 all read 0 but the five non-zero frames MOVE to k=6/36/66/96/126 (copy origin 8 -> 9) at 3235/3187/3151/3217/3082 px covering the whole changed card region y=12..132 — one frame-phase of content, not a 1-px seam. So the <=1/1/170 class is a PHASE-ALIGNMENT coincidence between our ~3.3-scanline whole copy (+ the pad) and canon's own mid-frame tile writes, and making the copy faster moves our writes EARLY rather than late. A wide alignment scan (canon base 0..83 x rust offset 150..300) found NO zero pairing near windowclose (min sampled ~95.6k), which also refutes 'it is just T116's stale align band'. CONSEQUENCE for the queue: neither wt/T112 (rank/zenny, cursor 7/6) nor wt/T117 (charged flash, cursor 10/9) can land on top of this change, so T119/T120 as written are dead — the judge's own successor T118b (anchor the seam phase to a scanline instead of a fitted iteration count) is now the gate for M2, M5 and M7. HAZARD recorded for the next person in that file: the first census build wrote VRAM BYTEWISE and corrupted the screenblock (GBA VRAM has no 8-bit stores — the byte lands in both halves of the halfword), which took cursor to 5.8M px; any copy_tiles edit must write u16/u32. Not done: root-level probe scripts ktable.py/shape.py are on the branch tip outside the ticket's Files list and must be moved to tools/ or dropped before any docs landing; the docs/coverage/cursor.md correction rides with T118b. Child 84 turns / 93 tools. Unverified: canon's own mid-frame write scanline (measured nowhere yet; --watch-write on canon's screenblock is the first job of T118b), and whether a timed writer at that scanline makes cursor AND windowclose footprint-stable.
**Why.** Three verified branches sit unmerged because of one mechanism, not three defects. `docs/coverage/cursor.md` records that `cursor`'s ≤1/1/170 class is "a layout-calibrated coincidence": agb's `commit()` copies the WHOLE screenblock (`vendor/agb/agb/src/display/tiled/screenblock.rs:42-47`, `copy_from_nonoverlapping(tiles.as_ptr(), self.size.num_tiles())`), which spills past vblank into the first visible scanlines, so on the frames where tile contents change (k=7/37/67/97/127, the five cursor moves) the cut shows as stale-vs-new pixels at a phase set by the binary's footprint. The absorber is `SEAM_PHASE_PAD_ITERS = 17` (`src/main.rs:322`, nop loop at :383-391, tagged `provenance: fitted`) and it has one-iteration (~1.5 px) granularity, which two footprints just beat: T112 (PARTIAL) swept 64→13/12, 96→10/9, 128→7/6, 144→88/56 and never reached ≤1/1; T117 (PARTIAL) swept 13:38/37, 14:11/11, 15:26/25, 16:10/9, 17:17/15, 18:20/19 for a +1132-byte asset. Canon has no such problem because it transfers only what changed, in 8-word chunks (`QueueEightWordAlignedGFXTransfer`, `sub_8001C94`, `asm/asm00_0.s:3752` per F37l). Until the copy itself is content-driven, every src/ landing needs a pad re-fit and M2's trace work stays off main.

**New evidence.** Postdates the two closed attempts at mid-frame placement (F35b BLOCKED, T21 BLOCKED — both were about *where* to put a copy at a chosen scanline; F37j NEGATIVE ported a backdrop drain and regressed cursor): after those closes T111 LANDED the pad (0fee41d) and, on top of it, T112's and T117's pad sweeps above are the first measurements of the pad's failure mode — no integer size exists for a feature-sized delta. The per-frame changed-chunk count has never been measured.

**Files.** `vendor/agb/agb/src/display/tiled/screenblock.rs` (the tile/map copy path — vendor edit justified in the report: the driver is what is wrong), `src/main.rs` (pad const + its VBlank closure: deleted or set to 0), `docs/coverage/cursor.md` (rewrite the coupling note with the new numbers), `docs/worklog/T118.md`. **NOT** tools/harness.py (row, canon side, negative, compare all frozen), tools/states.py, tools/trace.py, tools/oracle.py, tools/allowlist.py, src/battle.rs, reference/bn6f (read-only), canon.

2. Size the problem: for the cursor scenario, per frame, count the 8-word chunks that actually differ between our shadow screenblock and VRAM, and the chunks `commit()` writes today (1024 tiles × 4 chunks = 4096); convert both to cycles against the vblank budget (68 lines × 1128 = 76,704) and say whether a changed-chunk-only copy fits inside vblank on every one of the 170 frames. **measurement.**
3. Implement it: compare-and-copy per chunk (read-back or shadow compare — whichever the measurement supports), no scanline placement, no new pad; delete or zero `SEAM_PHASE_PAD_ITERS`. Rebuild; ROM sha256+size; fitted count before/after (main prints 17 today — it must go down). **code change + measurement.**

Content written to VRAM must be identical (only the volume of writes changes) — the other rows are the proof. Every new const cited or named. ≤8 captures, tool budget ≤80.

### T118b. cursor's seam phase anchored to a scanline instead of a fitted iteration count — the gate that lands wt/T112 (M2) and wt/T117 (M7)  *(NEGATIVE -- 2026-09-17, Docs landed 1e5b11e)*

**Result.** Docs landed 1e5b11e; no code merged (branch wt/t118b tip restores main's pad, anchor reverted; the 9-row read at the tip reproduces cursor 1/1/170/186279, fitted 17). TWO MODELS KILLED AND ONE DOOR OPENED. (1) The scanline anchor is dead on evidence: a REG_VCOUNT spin to LY=160 gives cursor 31/19 OUT on main's own footprint, LY=161 gives 37/21, and a 1..4 nop trim at LY=160 gives 31/19, 31/29, 31/29, 22/1 — no in-class value exists — because the phase that matters is SUB-scanline: 1 pad iteration ~= 6.5 cycles ~= 1.6 px of cut phase while one scanline ~= 190 iterations, and the anchor's own 28 bytes of code re-roll the footprint it was meant to survive. (2) The 'copy spills past vblank' model is dead (T118) and so is 'the pad puts us in class': at the fitted pad 17 the two sides' cuts COINCIDE (k=7/37/67/127 = 0 px, k=97 = 1 px, row y=5/x=183) — there is no band to measure in class; the band appears only on broken (footprint, pad) pairs and always has the same shape, scanlines 0..4 at x~217..238 (top-right corner), deepening as the pad leaves phase. (3) THE MEASUREMENT THAT MATTERS, footprint x pad -> cursor k37/k97 px (k7 = 0 everywhere): main 592536..592604 B: pad 0 = 0/1, pad 17 = 0/1; +1132 B static filler 595056..595140: pad 0 = 0/1, pad 13 = 0/1, pad 17 = 17/6, pad 21 = 25/3; -1132 B truncated asset 591408..591492: pad 0 = 0/1, pad 13 = 19/1, pad 17 = 40/1, pad 21 = 55/1. So **pad 0 is in class on all three footprints across a 3.6 KB span** while every nonzero pad is in class on exactly one — the pad is not the absorber, it is the thing that breaks the perturbed builds. That is a footprint-stable answer obtained by deletion, it retires a fitted constant (17 -> 16), and it is the route that unblocks wt/T117 (charged flash, +1132 B, 10/9 at pad 16) and any future src landing. T118b could not test it as a landing because its acceptance was written around installing an anchor and its capture budget went to the anchor sweep; T124 is that deletion test. Method notes carried for the next worker: the cursor row's fixture descriptor is NOT bare CARDNAME_ROW (it sets scroll/gauge/fire fields at +54..+7e) — the worker's first three captures used the wrong one and were discarded; pairing convention canon 15+k <-> rust origin+237+k with origin 8. Step 5 (apply to wt/T112 / wt/T117) was not run: no anchor value existed to apply. Unverified: canon's own mid-frame write scanline has never been timestamped (--watch-write on the screenblock is still the missing number), the 3.3-scanline copy duration is T118's cycle arithmetic rather than a scanline scan, and whether pad 0 holds beyond a ~3.6 KB delta. Child 21 turns / 26+ tools, ~$0.5.
**Why.** Two finished, verified branches sit unmerged on one veto. wt/T112 (M2 rank+zenny: `result` trace first divergence none, 40/40 both fields, row 0/0/40 held) reads cursor 7/6/170; wt/T117 (M7 charged muzzle flash: `buster_charge` 2498/188/32 → **188/188/32**, negative non-blind 3167, control `buster` 0/0/28) reads cursor 10/9/170. `docs/coverage/cursor.md` names the cause: agb's `GraphicsFrame::commit` copies the whole screenblock after vblank and spills into visible scanlines, so the cut's phase is a function of the binary's footprint; the landed absorber `SEAM_PHASE_PAD_ITERS` (src/main.rs:322, 382-391, T111 0fee41d) counts `nop` iterations at ~5-8 cycles each, so every code or asset delta re-rolls it. T117's sweep on a +1132-byte asset (13:38/37, 14:11/11, 15:26/25, 16:10/9, 17:17/15, 18:20/19) is non-monotone and reaches no ≤1/1 value at all. Until the knob's unit is a scanline, every later port of a routine is gated on a refit that may not exist.

**New evidence.** T56's code-size control test (NEGATIVE) established the class is footprint-calibrated, not logical; T111 (DONE 0fee41d) then proved a wait inside the registered VBlank closure moves the copy start with no loads or stores (verifier-confirmed timing-only). What did not exist then: two blocked footprints to test an anchor against, and the measurement that one integer count cannot serve both.

**Files.** `src/main.rs` (the VBlank closure: the pad and its replacement), `docs/coverage/cursor.md` (anchor + perturbation tables), `docs/worklog/T118.md`. **NOT** src/battle.rs, src/hudtiles.rs, tools/harness.py (row semantics frozen), tools/states.py, tools/trace.py, tools/oracle.py, tools/allowlist.py, reference/bn6f (read-only), canon.

**Do.**
1. On the k=37 seam frame measure both sides' cut: the scanline at which the tile copy is interrupted on canon and on ours at pad 17, and our copy's duration in scanlines (`probe.py` watch / per-scanline diff, one aligned capture pair). **measurement.**
2. Reproduce the defect on main's own binary: perturb the footprint (a declared `static [u8; N]`, +1132 B, and one in the other direction), sweep the pad over 4 sizes per footprint, report footprint × pad → cursor total/worst. This is the ticket's premise as numbers. ≤4 captures. **measurement.**
3. Anchor to hardware: in the same VBlank closure spin on `REG_DISPSTAT`'s LY count to a named target scanline before returning, so commit's copy starts at a scanline rather than at a cycle count. Name it `SEAM_ANCHOR_LY` with `// provenance:` citing step 1. If a sub-scanline `nop` trim is still needed, keep it and report it as fitted. **code change + measurement.**
4. Re-run cursor with ONE unchanged anchor value on three binaries whose footprints differ by ≥1 KiB (main, +1132 B, −1132 B or T117's real asset): ≤1/1/170 each, with the per-frame k table. **measurement.**
5. Apply only this change on top of wt/T112 and wt/T117 and report cursor's row line for each; then the 8-row guard set on main. **measurement.**

**Rules.** One anchor const, cited; a value that must change per footprint fails the ticket. `fitted` hygiene count must not rise (main prints 17). Row semantics and negatives frozen, no allowlist, no patch_sterile, canon never changes. ≤8 captures, tool budget ≤80.

**Acceptance.** cursor isolated ≤1/1/170 at one unchanged anchor across ≥3 footprints spanning ≥1 KiB, and ≤1/1/170 on wt/T112 and wt/T117 rebased with that same value — the two landings are the deliverable. verify_rows PASS on the 9-row set from a clean checkout is the veto. A bounded NEGATIVE closes it too: phase-vs-LY table, copy duration in scanlines, and what still sets the phase.

**Measure and report.** rows: cursor + 8-row guard set; frames 170/40. Measured cut scanline both sides, copy duration, footprint × pad table before, footprint × anchor table after, both branch reads, ROM sha256+size, fitted/derived/peeked before/after, commit; one line of mechanism; one line unverified (whether the anchor survives a >4 KiB delta).

**Coordinator:** owns src/main.rs only, so it pairs with T119/T120/T121/T122. Land it first, then wt/T112 and wt/T117 in the same session before dispatching further src work. Free tier: verify_rows on the 9-row set ×3 footprints; verifier for step 1's scanline measurement and step 3's cite. ≤$0.30 expected, ≤$0.60 cap. **Advances M2 and M7 — it is the gate on both their finished ports.**

THE MEASURED RULE (canon ROM watches, docs/coverage/emotion.md + docs/worklog/T123.md): 0x0203528F (eStruct2035280+0xf) is sub_801CC94's RNG-gated 12-frame BLINK countdown (arms 1-2 blinks, blanks the face only at countdown 5/6) and it reads flat 0x00 for 100 frames on the descriptor route; the face slot is +0x10/+0x11; the cross state is reachable at 0x0203ce2c (eBattleNaviStats0+0x2c, ZERO writes on the route) — poking 1 there holds the slot halfword at 0x0005 for the whole window, and the upload path's cmp #5 at asm00_2.s:27390 only skips blink bookkeeping (loc_801CBBE IS the upload), so a transformation-resolved slot-5 face is uploaded and drawn by canon. Also corrected: T122's claim that 0x0203528E reads 0 is wrong (it reads 0x03; the poke zeroes it mid-window, harmless there), and stacking Unk_32 on top of the transformation poke lands 0x000a via the synchro +5, not a cross face. WHY NOT A FIX (two independent blockers, both measured): (1) with src/emotion.rs's 5/6 arm deleted, emotion_cross (new scenario, slot 5 held) goes 51240/1281 -> 23480/587, i.e. our slot-5 face becomes pixel-exact and what remains is canon repainting MegaMan himself at a flat 587 px/frame in x~50..79/y~70..119 — the descriptor route fixes MegaMan calm, so the row would be a knowingly-boxed permanent red whose cause lives in src/battle.rs, outside this ticket's files, and it was NOT landed; (2) the same deletion breaks the FROZEN emotion_skip pairing row, 0/0/40 -> FAILED 27760/694 (negative 28404): same descriptor byte, contradictory outputs, src/fixture.rs untouchable, so no src/emotion.rs change satisfies both rows. emotion_syn and emotion_face_b stayed 0/0/40 with negative 644 under the fix (slots <5 are byte-identical). The supervisor ruled close-as-NEGATIVE over redefining a frozen row; the verified fix and the row definition remain on branch wt/t123 (evidence commits 1f1651f/7abf41c, revert 7130374, docs 4594f95) for the battle-side follow-up, whose subject the worklog names: M2/M7 MegaMan's cross-body model, the only thing that can take a cross-state emotion row to 0. SCOPE M7 emotion stays 2/25. fitted/derived/peeked 17/436/146 (no new fitted const; the fix only deleted an arm). Child 66 turns / 67 tools. Unverified: the other 20 slot values (only 0/2/4/5 have ever gone through a row), what writes 0x0203ce2c in a real cross activation, and whether the bit-1 blink-pattern copy at :27567-27574 is a second object our port does not model at all.
**Why.** T122 (PARTIAL, landed 3cf35ad) added `emotion_face_b` (0/0/40, non-blind 644) and, in the process, had its own mechanism claim **refuted by verifier-hyper** on a byte-exact read of `reference/bn6f` at 92705e0d. The landed `src/emotion.rs:71-74` and `:89-95` treat the descriptor's `emotion` value as if canon's draw gate suppressed the window for values 5/6, and build **no sprites** for those two values. The verifier's read says that is the wrong reason to be silent: `0x0203528F` (eStruct2035280+0xf) is not the face slot and not a per-slot gate — it is `sub_801CC94`'s **12-frame blink countdown** (`asm/asm00_2.s:27551` loads 0xc, `:27557-27566` decrements once per frame and reloads at 0, `:27567-27574` copies the 0x20-byte blink pattern `byte_801CDA4 -> byte_30016D0` off bit 1 of the same byte). The face slot is stored at **+0x10/+0x11** (`:27341-27342`, read back at `:27403`), and the upload path's `cmp #5` at `:27390` (`bge loc_801CBBE`) only skips the blink *bookkeeping* — `loc_801CBBE` **is** the upload. So a transformation-resolved slot 5 or 6 (`byte_801E700[1]=5`, `[2]=6`, the cross states) is uploaded and drawn on every frame **except** the two countdown phases that blank it. If our port draws nothing for those slots, a cross-state face is missing from the build and **no existing row can see it**: `emotion_syn`/`emotion_face_b`/`emotion_skip` all run behind the `enemies==0` gate with a zero transformation byte, which is exactly why T122's skip row measured an empty box against an empty box.

**New evidence.** None of this was available to T105 (DONE, landed 0fee41d), which wrote the 5/6 arm: the 25-entry `byte_801E700` transformation→slot table, the 23-entry `off_801CD08` deltas matching `FACE_INDEX`, and the per-slot palettes are now transcribed and verifier-confirmed on main in `docs/coverage/emotion.md`, and `assets/emotion.bin` is confirmed to carry all 23 slots' art and palettes (its 0x1900 bank equals ROM `dword_872D814`, its 736-byte palette run equals `dword_872F114`) — so the art for slots 5/6 exists in our build and simply is not being shown. T108 (NEGATIVE) separately measured that poking the gate byte to 5 makes the face vanish at 694 px/frame in x0..48/y18..34: that is the **blanking phase**, not a property of the slot.

**Files.** `src/emotion.rs` (the 5/6 arm and whatever the measurement replaces it with), `tools/states.py` (a scenario that reaches a non-zero transformation byte — the cross state — on the T105 descriptor route), `tools/harness.py` (one new row for it, same window shape as `emotion_syn`, with a negative), `docs/coverage/emotion.md` (the corrected mechanism section: countdown vs slot, with cites), `docs/worklog/T123.md`. **NOT** src/battle.rs, src/main.rs (T118b owns the seam there right now), vendor/, tools/trace.py, tools/oracle.py, tools/allowlist.py, tools/inventory.py, tools/chip_export.py, reference/bn6f (read-only), canon, `emotion_syn`/`emotion_face_b`/`emotion_skip`'s existing row definitions.

Reach a cross state on the descriptor route without touching battle code paths you don't own: find the byte the fixture already carries that `byte_801E700` indexes (the transformation), state where canon writes it, and build one scenario whose descriptor selects a slot in 5/6. Report whether the window can be held on that face for the row's 40 frames, and baseline the new row against unmodified `src/`. **measurement.**
3. Fix `src/emotion.rs` so the port reproduces step 1's rule for that slot — draw the face except on the blanking phases, art and palette from the landed asset (canon bytes, never a new colour) — and re-run: the new row 0/0/40 with a non-blind negative. **code change + measurement.**
4. Re-run `emotion_syn`, `emotion_face_b`, `emotion_skip` (all three must stay 0/0/40 with negative 644) and the 8-row guard set + `cursor`. If the new code changes the binary footprint and `cursor` leaves the ≤1/1/170 class, report it as the T118/T118b coupling it is — do not re-fit the pad, do not chase the tear, keep the branch unmerged and hand the numbers to T118b's landing. **measurement.**

(4) THE BOUNDED NEGATIVE HOLDS: both pointer tables span 0x80 = 32 entries and all 32 were enumerated, nonzero weakness exists ONLY at ai 0x19 (+6 = 0x40 at 0x08113754) and ai 0x1a (0x20 at 0x081143c8), struct1 Element(+5) is 0 for ALL 32 ai (the verifier's free strengthening fact: struct2's nibble is the only enemy element source), src/fixture.rs:360-361 has only KIND_METTAUR/KIND_GUNNER (both elem 0 / weak 0), primary row 0 is all-zero and weakness 0 fails both secondary branches -- no element-weak x!=1 pair is reachable on M3's fixtures without a RAM poke, so M3's first RULE row cannot be built from the existing fixtures; SCOPE CAVEAT: this is a negative about the ELEMENT PAIR only -- aura, elec-on-bubble and heat-on-grass ride the same r4 sum and can still fire on bubble/grass panels, so 'no x!=1 pair' must never be read as 'no x!=1 damage'; (5) PLUS (CHIP_ELEM_PLUS 0x4, constants.inc:121) and OBSTACLE (0x7, :124) have no cell in the 5x5 and no bit in {0x10,0x20,0x40,0x80}, and the 0x2000 secondary flag is unnamed for that field (the only 0x2000 struct_consts are OBJECT_FLAGS_BLIND and OBJECT_FLAGS_2_UNK_BIT_13 at loc 0x3c/0x40 -- the doc correctly refused to borrow BLIND's name). TWO DEFECTS CARRIED, neither fixed by this landing: (a) elements.md section 1's five inline asm38.s cites are shifted +2 to +12 (it puts mov r4,#1 at :3483, the bls at :3486/:3492 and mul at :3520; real :3480, :3483/:3487, :3508) -- the rule is right, the numbers need a docs commit; (b) the CHIP_ELEM->ELEM mapping (+1 and the secondary bit assignment) is DERIVED from table structure plus flag_enum, not from a measured conversion site (the worklog records the failed 11-byte table search) and must not be inherited as measured fact. Also: byte_3007444's VMA is IWRAM 0x03007444, so 0x081d7944 is a load-image location pinned by unique byte match + the .byte rows, not a live IWRAM read. ROM of the reference read: a37c1028 8388608 B. Fitted count not re-read -- no src change, main's 17 cannot have moved. Next pass: step 2's canon damage pair needs a RAM poke channel to give any defender a nonzero element, and docs/coverage/elements.md's cites need fixing.
**Why.** SCOPE M3 names "elements and weakness" and **no admitted ticket has touched it** (grep of TODO.md + TODO_ARCHIVE.md for `weakness` returns only the M3 prose line). Canon's rule is two IWRAM routines in the damage path: `getPrimaryElementWeaknessMultipler_3007432` (reference/bn6f/asm/asm38.s:3533-3550) and `getSecondaryElementWeaknessMultipler_30074e2` (asm38.s:3634-3692, whose r0 is documented "weakness bitfield" at :3632), both called at asm38.s:3483-3487 where the defender's byte is loaded with `ldrb r0, [r6,#oCollisionData_SecondaryElementWeakness]` (:3485) — that byte is filled from the enemy's own struct1 row (`enemy_getStruct1_struct_SecondaryElementWeakness = 0x06`, asm00_2.s:643; the store at asm00_2.s:21358). Fields are all named: `CollisionData.inc:89 PrimaryElement +0x2`, `:122 SecondaryElementWeakness +0x18`, `:123 SecondaryElement +0x19`, `BattleObject.inc:52 Element +0xe`, and the attacker's comes from the chip record (`ChipData.inc:9 u8 ChipElement // loc=0x6`). Our build reads no element in the damage path at all.

**New evidence.** `docs/recon/T10.md:22` maps the struct1 rows to their virus tables (`off_81090D0`, `MettaurEnemyStruct1_8109BD0` at idx 1, `GunnerEnemyStruct1_8112B94` at 0x5C), and T113/T121 read Struct2 rows out of ROM with no capture (`off_8109150[4]`'s row-0 halfword `elem_hp 0x005a` at 0x0810ae4c, stride 6) — the field's own name says element and HP ride in the same row, which is the first thing this ticket has to settle.

**Files.** `src/battle.rs` (the damage apply path only — not the spawn lines, not the charge arms), `src/actor.rs` or `src/objects.rs` (the defender's element/weakness bytes at object construction, from the identity/struct1 rows), `src/fixture.rs` (one descriptor field, only if step 2 needs an element on a fixture enemy), `tools/rom_enemy_tables.py` (the element/weakness columns of the struct1/Struct2 walk), `tools/states.py` (ONE scenario `elem_weak`), `tools/harness.py` (ONE row + its negative), `docs/coverage/elements.md` (NEW), `docs/worklog/T126.md`. **NOT** `src/main.rs`, `src/emotion.rs`, `tools/trace.py`'s judged set, `tools/oracle.py`, `tools/allowlist.py`, `reference/bn6f` (read-only), canon, any existing row's semantics.

1. No build, no capture: decode both multipliers' element→factor tables from the ROM bytes (cite every row) and state the secondary bitfield rule; then read out the element/weakness bytes for ai 1 (Mettaur) and ai 0x17 (Gunner) and settle whether Struct2's `elem_hp` halfword is element+HP or HP alone. **measurement.**

Baseline: ROM sha256+size; verify_rows re-reads the 43 chip rows + 8-row guard set + `result` at main's lines; then on canon, one capture of an element-bearing chip against a defender whose table says ≠×1, watching `oBattleObject_HP` (BattleObject.inc:105, +0x24) before and after the strike — report canon's damage number, ours' number for the same strike, and one ×1 control pair. **measurement.**

4. Add `elem_weak` and its row (negative = the same recipe against a ×1 defender, which must not be zero); run it: total/worst/frames, the negative's total, and `e1_hp`'s frame-by-frame match count both sides over those frames. **code change + measurement.**

### T127. M8's second results variant: the LOSER window as a scenario and a row  *(PARTIAL -- 2026-09-17, Worklog landed as 3f330a7 [--no-verify, docs only: no src/, vendor/, Cargo or assets/ path changed so no row a)*

STEPS 1-2 DONE, STEPS 3-5 NOT REACHED -- the tool budget went into the lever hunt (4 of 6 captures, 3 of them dead ends), and what came back is that the ticket's own premise about the lever was wrong. THE HP WORD IS NOT THE LEVER: --cheat 0x0203a9b4:0x0000 (HP forced 0) and :0x0001 (HP forced 1 so any hit kills) both leave the defeat gate eLinkState+0x48 (0x02009A18) unfired and the sequencer 0x0203CA70 parked at 0x08 across 300 and 400 frames, because deletion needs the damage path and the PAUSED battle's Mettaur never lands a hit on its own -- natural kill is a lottery, not a fixture. THE LEVER IS ONE POKE: --poke-at 40:0x0203a9b8:0x0008 (player CurState -> CUR_STATE_DESTROY) reaches sequencer 0x04000010 -- state 0x10, the lose end state, matching our port's own SEQ_10 transition at src/battle.rs:2889 -- within 5 frames and holds through 285. WHAT REMAINS FOR THE FOLLOW-UP, with three traps already written into docs/worklog/T127.md: the LOSER window's appearance frame, its >=40-frame stability and its pixels are unmeasured (the watch stopped at 285, before the window, which the win route's pacing puts 100-150 frames after the end state); the rust side must reach LOSER through the normal defeat path and NOT via start_state=1, because src/battle.rs:3193's fixture path hardcodes results::WIN and can never show the loser window; and the negative (same recipe plus DELETE_ENEMY) rests on a canon ending under both-dead that was never measured. Also flagged: canon's live Mettaur keeps animating behind the loser window, so residue there is an objects-side finding, not the LOSE arm. No src file touched, no ROM built, no row or scenario added; tools/states.py and tools/harness.py untouched. child $0.115, 53 turns.

THE MEASUREMENT THAT MATTERS MOST, and it is a correction to landed code: chip id is the ChipDataArr_8021DA8 array ORDINAL, and T46's landed family consts at src/battle.rs:917-923 were keyed to the wrong records (by library_num/id_sort_pos), so BOMB_SUB_BLKBOMB 0x01 actually names {55,56} and the 'CURSOR-element 0x06' story describes records the runtime never reads (ids 55/56/57 read chip_element 0x0a). THE FAILURE: panel_steal (new states.py scenario, bg ON, fixture 0x1B, window 90 = canon 5..94) reads FAILED 3190516/37634/90 with negative not blind 3241748; the frame-90 diffmask covers 98.01% of the screen (rows 0..159, cols 0..239) -- a whole-screen bg-on delta on the afterdissolve route, not the stolen column, i.e. the new scenario's canon/ours sides differ from the start, not the steal rule differing. UNVERIFIED: crack/break's live templates, what bit 0x4 is, which object delivers the nine one-frame writes (drip is one per 8 frames), which routine ORs init 0x2, and the poison-tick owner.
**Why.** T121b (NEGATIVE; exporter + the 13-word type->flag table landed `7d99c25`, stamped `b2c3586`) went to make `word_3007924` (ROM `0x081D7E24`, OR-ed into `oPanelData_Flags` by `_object_updatePanelParameters`, asm38.s:4205-4225 -- `lsl r2,r1,#2; ldr r3,[r3,r2]; orr r1,r3`, with `Alliance<<5` OR-ed separately) into `src/field.rs` data with a row on top. Its own measurement killed the plan, and a verifier pass (`13d8cd99`) has now separated what that means from what was merely asserted: **the live poison is not the table at all.** `object_panel_setPoison` (asm/object.s:2540-2563) writes its own template, guard then masked OR, confirmed instruction for instruction: `ldr r1,[r0,#0x14]; mov r2,#0x10; tst r1,r2; beq` (only a panel whose `Flags & 0x10` is zero is poisoned), `mov r2,#0x3f5f` (`dword_800CBD0`, :2561) `bic r1,r2`, `orr` with `0x114` (`off_800CBD4`, :2563) back into `[r0,#0x14]`, then one `mov r2,#4` feeding two `strb`s at `[r0,#2]` (`Type`) and `[r0,#6]` (`Animation`). The verifier checked the algebra by hand: `0x10032 & ~0x3f5f = 0x10020`, `| 0x114 = 0x10134`, and `~0x3f5f` clears `0x2` (blocks movement) and `0x10` while keeping `0x20` and `0x10000` -- so the landed type-4 word `0x110` is a *template* that differs from the live write by bit `0x4`, which this routine sets itself. Our field has no flag word at all (`src/field.rs:289 set` writes tile indices, `PANEL_CRACKED` :31 is a bool), so there is nothing for a trace to compare and M3 still has no rule -- while the transition is on screen: the doll's landing poisons its panel through `object_setPanelType` 4 at `src/battle.rs:346`/:1002, and a chip route poisons an area on canon.

The verifier re-ran the worklog's own recipe (`_chip_canon_0c("46")`, `--loadstate /tmp/afterdissolve_0x0c.state`, both `--poke-at 3` presses, `--zero BANNER_TILES`, `--raw=--disable-bg`) with `--watch 0x02039C00:0x320` for 260 frames and got rc=0 with an **empty value column** (`HDR: frame 0x02039c00`), so neither the occupancy bit nor `0x00010134` was readable; its 25- and 50-address per-panel forms both failed rc=1 inside `mgba_capture`. The raw capture is preserved at **`/tmp/t121b_wide.txt`** -- 261 frames, already on disk, no re-capture needed to interpret.

### T138. M3's super armor, ported for real: the boolean stat->flag copy, the 0x220000 gate, and a `super_armor` row that has never existed  *(OPEN -- 2026-09-18)*

**Why.** T132 measured that the rule is real and is one bit (verifier captures on the mettaur route: control HP 60->50 at canon frame 114 with CurAction 08->03, Timer 22 = `FLINCH_FRAMES` 0x17, recovering at 137; with the flag poked the HP sequence is IDENTICAL while CurAction STAYS 08 and Timer stays 0, the two sides differing on exactly frames 114..146), and then T132 merged nothing: its branch (wt/t132 @ 0d96154) is docs-only, its port was never written, and **the `super_armor` row has never existed** -- `grep -n super_armor tools/harness.py tools/states.py` is empty on main AND on wt/t132, and the branch's own worklog has the row only as a design (:132 "ONE row `super_armor` cloning the mettaur Check", :161 "expect 0/0/70"). So T132's reported `super_armor FAILED 944/61/32` is not reproducible from any committed fixture, and its excuse for not reaching 0 -- "the real divergence is at frames ~40-63, OUTSIDE the row's fixed 0..31 window" -- is self-refuting, because the third field of a harness line is the compared-frame count: 944 px inside 32 compared frames means the divergence is IN the window. T132 is therefore not finished, it is unstarted at steps 4-5, and the mechanism it wrote down is wrong in three places that a second verifier read out of the disassembly.

**The corrected mechanism (each line is a static read, re-check it in your first hour and cite what you find).**
- The site T132 called "the flinch gate" is NOT it. `sub_80178D4` (`asm00_2.s:18760-18772`) is entry 1 of a 3-word on-push action table (`asm31.s:107218`, one slot after `playerFlinchAction_80174FE+1 // on flinch // 0x03`): it tests **PARALYZED `0x800` FIRST** and then `dword_8017A94 = 0x20000`, and it writes **`CurAnim` +0x10** (0 / 1 / 2), never `CurAction` +0x9. It cannot by itself produce a preserved action id. Do not port it as a gate.
- The readers that actually test the bit in the hit path are `sub_801AEB0` (`asm00_2.s:23403-23411`) and `sub_801AF0E` (`:23451-23459`), and **both test `dword_801AF40 = 0x220000`** -- `OBJECT_FLAGS_SUPERARMOR` 0x00020000 (`CollisionData.inc:20`, bit 17 at `:38`) **OR `OBJECT_FLAGS_ANGER` 0x00200000** (`CollisionData.inc:24`). A bit-17-only port therefore reproduces the NaviCust route and not the anger route; say which one your row measures and do not claim both.
- The stat->flag copy is **boolean, not a product**: `sub_801393A` `asm00_2.s:10683-10691` is `mov r0,#1 / lsl r0,r0,#0x11 / mov r2,#0x23 / ldrb r1,[r6,r2] / tst r1,r1 / beq clear / bl object_setFlag1`. So the gate is `stat != 0`; a port that computes `stat << 17` mis-gates every stat above 1. (T132's report claimed the halfword "stores `value * 0x20000`" -- refuted by those four lines.) Its two `bl` callers are `asm00_2.s:10843` (inside `sub_80139C4`, which a per-frame navi update reaches, gated on HP!=0 / ActorType==PLAYER / AIIndex<=0xb / mood==2) and `asm03_0.s:15311`, so **"battle init only" is NOT established** -- settle when it runs, because that decides whether your row can poke a stat at all or must poke the flag.
- **No hit-path WRITER of bit 17 exists** anywhere in the disassembly: the named `OBJECT_FLAGS_SUPERARMOR_BIT` sites are the init copy, the `SetObjectSuperArmorFlag` family (`asm00_2.s:12396-12671`, the Transformation route, which is also what T132's own commit message says) and three clearers (`sub_8013C4E` :11058, `sub_8014080` :11645, `sub_80140EE` :11692, the last two also zeroing stat byte 0x23). Therefore T132's traced `0x00011032 -> 0x00031032` **at the hit frame** cannot be a ROM write of that bit: either the watch read a foreign word or the poke landed late. Re-measure it with `--watch` before writing any prose about who sets the flag.
- Poke hygiene, settled: the player's CollisionData on this route is 0x020384f0 + `ObjectFlags1` loc 0x3c = **0x0203852c** (low halfword) and **0x0203852e** (upper), and `--poke-at` writes halfwords, so the lever is `0x0203852e:0x0202` -- `0x0002` there CLEARS bit 25, which is pre-set (`0x02000000 OBJECT_FLAGS_AFFECTED_BY_ICE`) on this route, and would silently change a second flag. `0x02039b30`, in T132's report, appears in no file in this repo; drop it.
- Damage is unconditional and must stay that way: `object_subtractHP` is called at `asm00_2.s:24067` ahead of every flag test in that handler, and `sub_801B394` (`:24071-24105`) additionally tests `FlagsFromCollision & 0xc800002`, HP and Flags2 before setting `OBJECT_FLAGS_INVULNERABLE` and CurAction. Suppress the action, never the HP.
